Every action taken has the potential to alter, damage, or contaminate evidence. To preserve the integrity of the scene for as long as possible, investigators strictly follow a progression from the least-intrusive to the most-intrusive activities.\n\n- Least-Intrusive Activities (Done First): These are passive tasks that do not physically disturb the scene. They include taking written notes, wide-angle photography, video recording, and making initial sketches. \n\n- Most-Intrusive Activities (Done Last): These involve physical interaction, manipulation, and destruction. They include moving items, applying fingerprint powders or chemicals, cutting out drywall to extract bullets, and physically collecting items.\n\n❌ Why the Other Choices are Wrong\nA. most intrusive methods... first: This is completely backward. Latex is a natural plant-derived rubber. While it provides excellent flexibility, touch sensitivity, and a snug fit for picking up tiny pieces of evidence, it has severe chemical and physical weaknesses:\n\n- Chemical Vulnerability: Natural rubber has a weak chemical structure when it comes to petroleum-based hydrocarbons. If an investigator wears latex gloves while handling evidence from an arson or vehicle crime scene involving gasoline or kerosene, the liquid will rapidly penetrate the rubber, causing the glove to swell, distort, and melt away.\n\n- Environmental Vulnerability: Latex is highly sensitive to UV rays and thermal breakdown. If latex gloves are stored or exposed to prolonged, excessive heat or direct sunlight (such as being left in a crime scene responder's vehicle trunk or dashboard), the heat causes the rubber molecules to cross-link or dry out, making the gloves brittle, sticky, or completely useless.\n\n❌ Why the Other Choices are Wrong\n\nNitrile: This is a synthetic rubber engineered specifically to replace latex. Nitrile provides excellent resistance to petroleum products like gasoline, diesel fuel, and kerosene. It also features much higher thermal stability and puncture resistance.\n\nNeoprene: This is another heavy-duty synthetic rubber. It is widely used because it maintains its physical flexibility across a wide range of extreme hot and cold temperatures and is highly resistant to degradation from hazardous fuels and solvents.\n\nPowder-free gloves: This term refers to a manufacturing finish (removing the cornstarch powder inside to prevent skin allergies and evidence contamination), not a raw base material. A powder-free glove can be made of nitrile or neoprene, which would not degrade under these conditions." id : 1788867128533 question : "In using gloves for hand protection in collection of evidence, this glove material will swell and degrade if exposed to gasoline or kerosene. [100 … 190] 100 : {id: 1788879116047,…} correct : "Greenstick Fracture" distractors : ["Linear Fracture", "Spiral Fracture", "Pathologic Fracture"] 0 : "Linear Fracture" 1 : "Spiral Fracture" 2 : "Pathologic Fracture" explanation : "💡 Why Answer A is Correct\nIn forensic medicine and legal medicine, a greenstick fracture is an incomplete break.\n\n- The Analogy: Think of trying to break a fresh, living \"green\" branch off a tree. If you bend it, it will not snap cleanly in half. Instead, the outer side will crack open under the tension, while the inner side just bends and stays connected.\n\n- The Injury: In the human body, this happens most commonly in young children because their bones are soft, flexible, and still growing. When they suffer a blunt force injury or fall, the bone bends—breaking on only one side while the other side remains intact.\n\n❌ Why the Other Choices are Wrong\nB. Linear Fracture: This is a fracture where the break forms a straight, thin line that runs parallel to the long axis of the bone. It is common in skull injuries (often called hairline fractures) where the bone cracks but does not move out of place or bend.\n\nC. Spiral Fracture: This is a severe, complete break caused by a twisting or rotational force (like a leg getting violently twisted during an assault or accident). The break winds around the bone like a corkscrew.\n\nD. Pathologic Fracture: This is a bone break caused by an underlying disease or medical condition (like osteoporosis, bone cysts, or cancer) that makes the bone incredibly weak. It happens during normal, everyday movements rather than from a specific violent impact." id : 1788879116047 question : "A fracture wherein only one side of the bone is broken while the other side is merely bent." 101 : {id: 1788879197343,…} correct : "Exhumation" distractors : ["Interment", "Autopsy", "Inhumation"] 0 : "Interment" 1 : "Autopsy" 2 : "Inhumation" explanation : "💡 Why Answer B is Correct\nIn forensic and legal medicine, exhumation (also known as disinterment) is the legal and physical process of digging up a buried corpse from its grave.\nThis is usually ordered by a court or investigators when new evidence arises after a person has already been buried. For example, if a death was initially ruled a natural heart attack, but later a witness claims the victim was poisoned, investigators will exhume the body to perform a forensic examination or a delayed autopsy to uncover the true cause of death.\n\n❌ Why the Other Choices are Wrong\nA. While an autopsy is performed after an exhumation, the physical act of removing the body from the ground is not called an autopsy.\n\nD. There is zero potential for recovery of cognitive functions.\n\n❌ Why the Other Choices are Wrong\nA. Sociologic death: This is a psychosocial concept, not a medical condition. It occurs when a person is isolated, abandoned, or treated by society as if they are dead (such as individuals with severe advanced dementia or those cast out of a community), even though their body and brain are still fully alive.\nB. Psychic death: This refers to a psychological state where a person completely gives up on life, often due to severe trauma, extreme grief, or profound depression. They become entirely unresponsive to their surroundings mentally, but their physical brain and organs function perfectly fine without life support.\nD. In a standard line-up, a real witness looks at a group of similar-looking people to see if they recognize the actual perpetrator.\n\nStern approach: This is a direct, no-nonsense interrogation style where the investigator acts completely authoritative, harsh, and dead-serious. time", "Saving faces"] 0 : "Simplicity of the question" 1 : "One question at a time" 2 : "Saving faces" explanation : "💡 Why \"Accept implied answer\" is the Correct Choice (The \"NOT\" rule)\n\nIn criminal investigation and police questioning fundamentals, an investigator must explicitly avoid accepting implied answers. \n\nWhat is an Implied Answer? An implied answer is a non-verbal response, like a simple nod of the head, a shrug of the shoulders, or an ambiguous facial expression. \n\nThe Forensic Rule: In a proper police investigation, a witness or suspect's answers must be completely oral, clear, detailed, and direct. Non-verbal clues can easily be misunderstood, cannot be documented accurately in a written affidavit, and do not hold up cleanly in a court room. Therefore, accepting an implied answer is strictly against standard questioning rules. \n\n❌ Why the Other Choices are Wrong\n\nChoices 2, 3, and 4 are all standard, universally mandated rules that an investigator must strictly follow during an interview or interrogation:\n\nSimplicity of the question: Questions must be framed in simple, everyday language. Investigators must avoid confusing legal terms or technical jargon so the subject fully understands what is being asked. \n\nOne question at a time: The investigator must never bundle multiple queries together (e.g., \"Where were you last night and who did you see and what time did you leave?\"). Asking one short, simple question at a time prevents confusion and stops the subject from selectively ignoring pieces of the question. \n\nSaving faces: If a subject makes an obvious exaggeration, a minor mistake about a specific time, or an error regarding distances, the investigator should avoid aggressively calling them a liar. Instead, they work with the subject to gently correct the facts while letting them \"save face,\" which keeps them cooperative and prevents them from shutting down emotionally." id : 1788960809758 question : "Which of the following is NOT among the rules to be observed in questioning a suspect?" title : "CDI 3" 3 : {id: "quiz_1788523342096", title: "CDI 4",…} id : "quiz_1788523342096" questions : [{id: 1788523648118,…}, {id: 1788523776384,…}, {id: 1788523961411,…}, {id: 1788524048130,…},…] 0 : {id: 1788523648118,…} correct : "The privilege of immediate use of particular section of highway" distractors : ["The personal right to move from one place to another",…] 0 : "The personal right to move from one place to another" 1 : "The way or place open to the motorist and pedestrian" 2 : "The right of a vehicle to occupy a space w/c at the time is occupied by another vehicle" explanation : "" id : 1788523648118 question : "A number of traffic regulations revolve around questions of right of way, for practically purposes, one of the following is the best definition of \"Right of way\"." 1 : {id: 1788523776384,…} correct : "I, II, IV" distractors : ["I, II, III", "I, III, IV", "I, II, III, IV"] 0 : "I, II, III" 1 : "I, III, IV" 2 : "I, II, III, IV" explanation : "" id : 1788523776384 question : "It is unlawful for any person to drive a two (2)- wheeled motorcycle with a child on board on public roads where there is heavy volume of vehicles unless. trafficking\"—which refer to illegal trade and business, not cars stuck on a highway.
